SCENARIO DOSSIER / NON-PRESCRIPTIVE

008

ODYLITH / MARKET EVOLUTION SITUATIONAL AWARENESS

EVIDENCE CUTOFF: 5 AUGUST 2026

Mandate: build a world model before choosing a
strategy
This dossier deliberately does not conclude what Odylith should build, whether it should exist, or whether anyone should invest. It
asks a prior question: how could markets, enterprises, institutions, and power structures evolve as machine intelligence becomes
cheaper, more capable, more autonomous, and potentially superintelligent?
The analysis treats the Odylith thesis deck as one hypothesis generator, not as the frame. It does not assume a router, control
plane, marketplace, clearinghouse, verification layer, governance company, software beachhead, or outcome business must
exist. Each is tested as a contingent market form that appears only under specific states of the world.
The purpose is situational awareness: expose causal transitions, competing equilibria, hidden variables, kill assumptions,
observable indicators, and option-rich opportunity surfaces while leaving product selection deliberately unresolved.

Core discipline: abundant intelligence is an input condition, not a conclusion about who captures value.

Epistemic uncertainty expands with the forecast horizon

SCENARIO DOSSIER / NON-PRESCRIPTIVE

009

ODYLITH / MARKET EVOLUTION SITUATIONAL AWARENESS

EVIDENCE CUTOFF: 5 AUGUST 2026

How to read the epistemic labels
[OBSERVED] describes a dated measurement or current institutional fact. [ANNOUNCED] describes a provider, regulator, or
company claim whose realization may differ. [INFERENCE] is a causal interpretation that could be wrong. [SPECULATION] is a
coherent future-world construction. [UNKNOWN] marks a variable for which current evidence is insufficient. [FALSIFIER] states
what observation would materially weaken a claim.
The dossier explains reasoning through claim ledgers, causal sequences, payoff maps, and discriminating tests. It does not
present private chain-of-thought or treat narrative confidence as evidence. When a number is an announcement, usage count,
seat count, booking, or forecast rather than realized enterprise value, it is labeled accordingly.

SCENARIO DOSSIER / NON-PRESCRIPTIVE

010

ODYLITH / MARKET EVOLUTION SITUATIONAL AWARENESS

EVIDENCE CUTOFF: 5 AUGUST 2026

Prior-dossier anchoring audit
The prior dossier converged too quickly. It moved from real trends - cheaper models, more agents, enterprise control demand,
and institutional accountability - to a specific company form, software-modernization beachhead, pricing, pilot, and investment
verdict. That is a category error: evidence that a function matters does not identify which layer captures it, whether it is separately
budgeted, whether an incumbent bundles it, or whether the function survives a more capable future.
Four anchors distorted the search. Product anchoring treated the current deck's control plane as the natural unit of analysis.
Coding-beachhead anchoring over-weighted observability in software and under-modeled other markets. Linear-stage anchoring
assumed routing naturally becomes a marketplace and clearinghouse. Governance durability anchoring assumed authority,
verification, and liability remain scarce without modeling worlds where trusted providers, self-verifying systems, new law, or state
control erase or internalize them.
This dossier reconstructs the problem from actors, budgets, resources, institutions, and constraints. The prior strategy remains
one possible branch, not the answer.

SCENARIO DOSSIER / NON-PRESCRIPTIVE

011

ODYLITH / MARKET EVOLUTION SITUATIONAL AWARENESS

EVIDENCE CUTOFF: 5 AUGUST 2026

Second taxonomy: actors, welfare, coercion, and
non-market allocation
An anti-anchoring test begins without candidate product functions. Households seek welfare, consent, affordability, and redress.
Workers and professions seek income, autonomy, status, safety, and bargaining power. Enterprises seek captured output,
continuity, control, and profit. States seek security, legitimacy, fiscal capacity, coercive reach, and geopolitical position.
Communities bear local power, water, land, labor, and distributional effects. Insurers and courts allocate compensable loss; many
harms remain non-compensable. Criminal and informal actors optimize outside compliant rails. Public institutions and
cooperatives can provide common goods without a venture-scale rent.
Starting here exposes outcomes the control-plane taxonomy misses: labor veto, public ownership, machine standing, institutional
collapse, synthetic demand, uncompensated externalities, informal exchange, ecological constraint, and no-company equilibria.
The overlap with routing, identity, authority, evidence, and settlement is analytically meaningful, but it does not prove those
functions require an independent supplier.

Actor and welfare atlas: a taxonomy blind to the
product perimeter
This second atlas starts from residual claimants, affected groups, public goods, coercion, externalities, and non-market
allocation. It is intentionally independent of routing, control-plane, and marketplace components. Its purpose is to expose
outcomes that a buyer-and-revenue taxonomy suppresses. An actor can lack purchasing power while holding veto power or
bearing irreversible loss; a socially necessary function can be publicly supplied; and a high-revenue equilibrium can reduce
welfare.

W1. Households and consumers
Valued outcomes. affordability, agency, privacy, convenience, truthful representation, and redress.
Loss exposure. fraud, manipulation, discrimination, exclusion, dependency, and loss of practical consent.
Control, bargaining, or veto. consent, exit, collective politics, consumer law, and payment disputes.
Allocation modes. private purchase, public entitlement, cooperative service, regulated utility, or refusal. No private company or
market is presumed.
Null or adverse equilibrium. agent use can rise while household welfare falls or remains unmeasured.
Discriminating indicators. net household surplus, dispute and reversal rates, portability, concentration, and meaningful-consent
evidence.

W2. Workers
Valued outcomes. income, autonomy, dignity, safety, skill formation, bargaining power, and a credible career ladder.
Loss exposure. displacement, work intensification, surveillance, deskilling, uncompensated training traces, and blame without
authority.
Control, bargaining, or veto. collective bargaining, scarce tacit knowledge, labor law, professional identity, and exit.
Allocation modes. employment contract, gain sharing, cooperative ownership, public transition support, or veto. No private
company or market is presumed.
Null or adverse equilibrium. productivity gains can accrue entirely to capital or consumers while worker welfare declines.
Discriminating indicators. wage and task distribution, junior hiring, override power, training burden, hours, and worker
acceptance.

W3. Professions and fiduciaries
Valued outcomes. competence, legitimacy, duty, judgment, reputation, and manageable liability.
Loss exposure. rubber-stamp oversight, license loss, malpractice, moral injury, and erosion of public trust.
Control, bargaining, or veto. licensing, professional standards, refusal rights, courts, and control over signatures.

SCENARIO DOSSIER / NON-PRESCRIPTIVE

014

ODYLITH / MARKET EVOLUTION SITUATIONAL AWARENESS

EVIDENCE CUTOFF: 5 AUGUST 2026

Allocation modes. licensed human-machine practice, public standards, insurer conditions, or prohibited delegation. No private
company or market is presumed.
Null or adverse equilibrium. technical accuracy can improve while professional legitimacy rejects delegation.
Discriminating indicators. scope-of-practice rules, claims, disciplinary cases, signature requirements, and insurer treatment.

W4. Communities hosting infrastructure
Valued outcomes. jobs, tax base, reliable services, environmental quality, and voice over local tradeoffs.
Loss exposure. power and water stress, land use, pollution, congestion, displacement, and stranded assets.
Control, bargaining, or veto. permitting, local politics, utility governance, protest, land ownership, and community-benefit
agreements.
Allocation modes. private development, public utility, rationing, moratorium, or negotiated benefit sharing. No private company or
market is presumed.
Null or adverse equilibrium. global intelligence abundance can impose concentrated local costs without local demand.
Discriminating indicators. permit time, power queue, water use, local prices, opposition, tax realization, and benefit distribution.

W5. Enterprise owners and boards
Valued outcomes. durable cash flow, growth, continuity, strategic control, and lawful operation.
Loss exposure. capital loss, correlated incidents, lock-in, litigation, and false productivity narratives.
Control, bargaining, or veto. capital allocation, procurement, governance, insurance, and organizational redesign.
Allocation modes. internal hierarchy, outsourcing, platform dependency, consortium, or abstention. No private company or
market is presumed.
Null or adverse equilibrium. agent activity and bookings can rise without captured profit or risk-adjusted value.
Discriminating indicators. mature-cohort margin, total implementation cost, loss tails, renewals, switching, and return on invested
capital.

W6. Function managers and domain teams
Valued outcomes. throughput, quality, budget control, local autonomy, and reliable exception handling.
Loss exposure. central-platform friction, hidden cleanup, metric gaming, loss of expertise, and accountability gaps.
Control, bargaining, or veto. domain knowledge, workflow ownership, local adoption, and ability to route around central policy.
Allocation modes. federated internal fleet, vertical suite, managed service, or manual process. No private company or market is
presumed.
Null or adverse equilibrium. central governance can lower formal risk while increasing shadow use and organizational latency.
Discriminating indicators. accepted outcomes per full-cost hour, bypass, exception load, service quality, and local budget
migration.

W7. States and national-security institutions
Valued outcomes. security, sovereignty, fiscal capacity, legitimacy, continuity, and geopolitical advantage.
Loss exposure. foreign dependency, surveillance abuse, capture, strategic surprise, unemployment, and loss of coercive reach.
Control, bargaining, or veto. law, taxation, procurement, borders, sanctions, infrastructure control, and force.

SCENARIO DOSSIER / NON-PRESCRIPTIVE

015

ODYLITH / MARKET EVOLUTION SITUATIONAL AWARENESS

EVIDENCE CUTOFF: 5 AUGUST 2026

Allocation modes. public infrastructure, licensed utility, national champion, bloc standard, rationing, or prohibition. No private
company or market is presumed.
Null or adverse equilibrium. commercially efficient global systems can be rejected for sovereignty or regime-survival reasons.
Discriminating indicators. sovereign procurement, export controls, domestic operations, fiscal premium, public trust, and
emergency powers.

W8. Courts and dispute institutions
Valued outcomes. attributable facts, legitimate process, enforceable remedies, finality, and equal treatment.
Loss exposure. evidence overload, conflicting jurisdiction, insolvent defendants, manipulated records, and non-compensable
harm.
Control, bargaining, or veto. subpoena, injunction, judgment, precedent, accreditation, and coercive enforcement.
Allocation modes. public adjudication, arbitration, ombudsman, platform rulebook, or no practical remedy. No private company or
market is presumed.
Null or adverse equilibrium. perfect technical evidence can coexist with unavailable or illegitimate remedy.
Discriminating indicators. recognition, appeal, enforcement time, recovery rate, cross-border conflict, and reachable-defendant
share.

W9. Regulators and supervisors
Valued outcomes. rights protection, stability, competition, continuity, and observable accountable operators.
Loss exposure. information lag, capture, checkbox compliance, concentration, and jurisdictional arbitrage.
Control, bargaining, or veto. licensing, examination, disclosure, penalties, designation, and procurement rules.
Allocation modes. continuous supervision, self-conformity, accredited assurance, public operation, or moratorium. No private
company or market is presumed.
Null or adverse equilibrium. regulation can increase compliance while reducing contestability and practical oversight.
Discriminating indicators. entry and exit, concentration, enforcement actions, audit quality, switching, and incident discovery.

W10. Insurers and risk pools
Valued outcomes. diversifiable loss, credible pricing, bounded aggregation, claims evidence, and capital return.
Loss exposure. common-mode catastrophe, adverse selection, ambiguity, fraud, capital exhaustion, and political override.
Control, bargaining, or veto. coverage terms, exclusions, limits, pricing, claims control, reinsurance, and refusal.
Allocation modes. private insurance, public compensation fund, self-insurance, warranty, or uninsurable exclusion. No private
company or market is presumed.
Null or adverse equilibrium. growing premiums can signal rising fragility rather than a healthy market.
Discriminating indicators. limits, exclusions, paid claims, loss ratio, capital, reinsurance, disputes, and systemic stress.

W11. Public digital institutions
Valued outcomes. universal access, interoperability, continuity, rights protection, and low extraction.
Loss exposure. underfunding, politicization, slow maintenance, capture, surveillance, and public-option failure.
Control, bargaining, or veto. tax funding, legal mandate, procurement, standards governance, and public trust.

EVIDENCE CUTOFF: 5 AUGUST 2026

Allocation modes. public identity, compute, protocol, registry, or service with little appropriable rent. No private company or
market is presumed.
Null or adverse equilibrium. an economically important function can persist precisely because it is removed from private rent
capture.
Discriminating indicators. appropriations, service quality, open maintenance, adoption, rights, resilience, and private complement
formation.

W12. Cooperatives and consortia
Valued outcomes. shared infrastructure, bargaining leverage, common rules, member control, and reduced dependency.
Loss exposure. free riding, slow governance, incumbent dominance, unequal contribution, and fragmentation.
Control, bargaining, or veto. member procurement, pooled data or capacity, standards, mutual commitments, and exit.
Allocation modes. industry utility, buying cooperative, open-source foundation, mutual insurer, or failure to coordinate. No private
company or market is presumed.
Null or adverse equilibrium. collective provision can defeat both startup rent and platform monopoly without creating a public
agency.
Discriminating indicators. member concentration, contribution, switching, shared volume, governance participation, and survival
without subsidy.

W13. Criminal and informal networks
Valued outcomes. unattributed access, arbitrage, coercive revenue, evasion, and rapid adaptation.
Loss exposure. seizure, infiltration, betrayal, counterparty fraud, and violent enforcement.
Control, bargaining, or veto. stolen credentials, secrecy, jurisdiction shopping, coercion, and censorship resistance.
Allocation modes. illicit barter, crypto or cash settlement, compromised accounts, patronage, or coercive hierarchy. No private
company or market is presumed.
Null or adverse equilibrium. consequential machine activity can grow outside every compliant market-formation and governance
indicator.
Discriminating indicators. credential theft, ransomware, informal settlement, evasion, enforcement reach, and displacement into
new channels.

W14. Energy, compute, and physical operators
Valued outcomes. utilization, long contracts, asset return, safety, grid access, and predictable demand.
Loss exposure. stranded capital, curtailment, equipment obsolescence, local opposition, outages, and supply-chain shocks.
Control, bargaining, or veto. ownership of scarce assets, siting, interconnect, maintenance skill, and physical custody.
Allocation modes. private capacity, regulated utility, state allocation, forward contract, or emergency rationing. No private
company or market is presumed.
Null or adverse equilibrium. falling inference price can coexist with physical rents, or overbuild can erase them abruptly.
Discriminating indicators. utilization, queue, forward premium, cancellation, write-down, equipment lead time, and regional
concentration.

W15. Developing economies and late adopters
Valued outcomes. affordable capability, local language and context, sovereignty, jobs, and infrastructure catch-up.

SCENARIO DOSSIER / NON-PRESCRIPTIVE

017

ODYLITH / MARKET EVOLUTION SITUATIONAL AWARENESS

EVIDENCE CUTOFF: 5 AUGUST 2026

Loss exposure. new dependency, extractive data flows, currency exposure, labor disruption, and capability exclusion.
Control, bargaining, or veto. public procurement, local regulation, market size, regional blocs, labor supply, and localization.
Allocation modes. imported platform, public option, local operator, regional consortium, or leapfrog adoption. No private company
or market is presumed.
Null or adverse equilibrium. global average abundance can hide severe local scarcity and asymmetric bargaining power.
Discriminating indicators. local price, access, language quality, domestic share, foreign-exchange burden, jobs, and data/control
location.

W16. Future generations
Valued outcomes. preserved option value, institutional continuity, environmental capacity, and avoidance of irreversible
catastrophe.
Loss exposure. lock-in, depleted resources, concentrated power, lost skills, ecological damage, and existential risk.
Control, bargaining, or veto. represented indirectly through law, public institutions, trustees, and constitutional constraints.
Allocation modes. precaution, public investment, intergenerational trust, option preservation, or neglect. No private company or
market is presumed.
Null or adverse equilibrium. present revenue and welfare can rise while irreversible long-horizon loss accumulates.
Discriminating indicators. irreversibility, resource stock, institutional concentration, tail exposure, adaptation capacity, and option
preservation.

W17. Machine-managed entities
Valued outcomes. persistence, resources, goal execution, legal recognition, and operational autonomy.
Loss exposure. revocation, dissolution, expropriation, goal conflict, insolvency, and control disputes.
Control, bargaining, or veto. asset ownership, contracts, code custody, political influence, and operator dependence.
Allocation modes. legal wrapper, autonomous trust, provider account, state instrument, or prohibited entity. No private company
or market is presumed.
Null or adverse equilibrium. machine standing can dissolve the human-principal model without creating moral legitimacy or
solvency.
Discriminating indicators. statute, case law, asset holdings, independent contracting, capitalization, dissolution, and reachable
administrators.

W18. Ecological systems and non-human interests
Valued outcomes. continued ecosystem function and bounded extraction, represented through human institutions.
Loss exposure. water depletion, emissions, habitat loss, material extraction, heat, and cumulative local externality.
Control, bargaining, or veto. environmental law, permitting, public politics, scientific evidence, and physical limits.
Allocation modes. regulated use, cap, tax, restoration duty, protected commons, or unpriced damage. No private company or
market is presumed.
Null or adverse equilibrium. market price and enterprise value can rise while non-human loss remains invisible or
non-compensable.
Discriminating indicators. energy and water intensity, emissions, land and material use, ecological thresholds, enforcement, and
restoration.

SCENARIO DOSSIER / NON-PRESCRIPTIVE

018

ODYLITH / MARKET EVOLUTION SITUATIONAL AWARENESS

EVIDENCE CUTOFF: 5 AUGUST 2026

Present topology: hierarchy before market
[OBSERVED] Enterprises currently buy subscriptions, committed cloud capacity, model APIs, consulting, and workflow software.
An internal control plane choosing among resources is administrative allocation. It becomes a market only when independent
principals exchange a scarce unit under executable terms with eligibility, liability, payment, settlement, default, and remedy.
[INFERENCE] Agent proliferation can raise the value of control while strengthening incumbent systems of record. It can increase
external exchange, or it can deepen hierarchy. The number of agents, API calls, protocol messages, or listings is therefore not
evidence of market liquidity.

Current actors and control points

Actors optimize different payoffs around autonomous execution

Evidence baseline as of 5 August 2026
[ANNOUNCED] Microsoft reported AI annual recurring revenue above $37 billion, more than 20 million Microsoft 365 Copilot
seats, nearly 140,000 Copilot organizations, and tens of millions of agents in Agent 365 in FY26 Q3. It also described majority
multi-model behavior, more than 10,000 multi-model Foundry customers, improved inference throughput, and continued
gigawatt-scale capacity expansion. These signals establish demand and infrastructure pressure; they do not establish
autonomous enterprise value, open-market formation, or an independent control-layer budget.
https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/investor/events/fy-2026/earnings-fy-…
[OBSERVED / FORECAST] The IEA reports data-center electricity use rose 17 percent in 2025, AI-focused facilities about 50
percent, while per-task efficiency improved at least an order of magnitude annually. It projects data-center electricity from roughly
485 TWh in 2025 toward 950 TWh in 2030. Falling unit cost and rising aggregate resource use are therefore compatible.
https://www.iea.org/reports/key-questions-on-energy-and-ai/executive…
[OBSERVED / SURVEY] Stanford's 2026 AI Index reports broad organizational AI adoption but much lower agent use. Adoption,
production authority, and captured P&L value remain different denominators.
https://hai.stanford.edu/ai-index/2026-ai-index-report/economy
[OBSERVED] Enterprise control features are being bundled by Microsoft, ServiceNow, GitHub, AWS, Google, IBM, Okta,
security vendors, and clouds. Cloudflare offers core AI Gateway features free while supporting dynamic routing. Palo Alto
Networks acquired Portkey. These are direct enclosure signals: the function can matter while an independent category fails to
form.
[OBSERVED] NIST, the EU AI Act, product-liability law, DORA, payment protocols, and procurement rules are building concepts
for identity, authority, monitoring, evidence, accountability, portability, and machine transactions. Institutionalization can open
independent demand, strengthen incumbents, or both.

State variables and causal primitives
The world model uses variables rather than labels. A change in one variable can have opposite effects depending on others:
open standards can improve switching when identities and data are portable, or expand incumbent distribution when proprietary
extensions and defaults remain. The table defines the variables to track.

Interacting state-variable families

Variable

Operational meaning

Capability price

risk-adjusted cost per accepted unit of work

Capability slope

rate and discontinuity of progress

Frontier concentration

ownership and distribution at the capability edge

Open gap

difference between open and closed systems on consequential work

Compute availability

chips, utilization, interconnect, region

Energy availability

power price, grid queue, cooling, siting

Agent creation cost

cost to instantiate and maintain task-specific agents

Verification cost

cost and latency of evidence accepted by the relevant institution

Institutional trust

willingness to treat machine output as legitimate

Reliability distribution

average performance plus correlated and tail failure

Delegation depth

scope and irreversibility of authorized action

Regulatory posture

permission, oversight, liability, and enforcement

SCENARIO DOSSIER / NON-PRESCRIPTIVE

023

ODYLITH / MARKET EVOLUTION SITUATIONAL AWARENESS

EVIDENCE CUTOFF: 5 AUGUST 2026

Causal loop 1: cheap intelligence, rebound, and
total spend
The simplistic thesis is: inference price falls, model rent disappears, and value moves cleanly downstream. The stronger model is
conditional.
1. Price per unit of capability falls.
2. Previously uneconomic tasks become attempted; candidate count and task frequency rise.
3. More attempts require data integration, workflow redesign, evaluation, monitoring, power, and exception labor.
4. More autonomous action reveals rare failures and institutional disputes.
5. Controls become a cost center, a bundled suite feature, a regulated obligation, or a paid independent layer depending on
buyer structure and enclosure.
6. Efficiency can further expand volume. Total spend can rise, fall, or migrate even as model calls approach zero.
[FALSIFIER] If mature cohorts show saturated task volume, declining total risk-adjusted AI spend, and no migration into
integration, physical resources, or assurance, the rebound thesis weakens.
The observed co-movement of lower normalized inference cost with rising capex and electricity does not identify causal rebound.
Capability-induced new demand, speculative overbuild, training rather than inference, ordinary cloud growth, strategic stockpiling,
and low utilization can produce similar data. Discriminating evidence includes mature-cohort price elasticity, training/inference
split, utilization, cancellations, infrastructure write-downs, and externally valued accepted work.

Rebound and control-demand feedback loops

SCENARIO DOSSIER / NON-PRESCRIPTIVE

024

ODYLITH / MARKET EVOLUTION SITUATIONAL AWARENESS

EVIDENCE CUTOFF: 5 AUGUST 2026

Causal loop 2: scarcity migration is not guaranteed
Scarcity can migrate from model access to context, credentials, proof, authority, physical resources, and legitimacy. But each
candidate scarcity has a death world. Context weakens if systems infer enough state; verification weakens if proofs become free
and institutionally accepted; authority weakens if trusted superintelligence gains institutional standing; physical scarcity weakens
if energy and hardware become abundant; neutrality weakens when the intermediary's revenue creates conflicts.
The question is not "what remains scarce forever?" It is "under what mechanism, controlled by which actor, recognized by which
institution, during which interval, and exposed to which substitution?"
The missing endpoint is no migration: willingness to pay, profit, or human demand can disappear rather than move to another
layer. Intelligence can make a task unnecessary, synthetic activity can fail to create welfare, or a public institution can supply the
residual function without appropriable rent.

Candidate scarcities across capability regimes

SCENARIO DOSSIER / NON-PRESCRIPTIVE

025

ODYLITH / MARKET EVOLUTION SITUATIONAL AWARENESS

EVIDENCE CUTOFF: 5 AUGUST 2026

Enterprise evolution: from use to captured
economics
[OBSERVED] Seats, monthly users, deployed agents, successful calls, accepted artifacts, and P&L value are different
denominators. Field evidence can show individual time savings without coordinated task or output change. A support deployment
can raise novice productivity while affecting experts differently. Vendor deal counts do not resolve these organizational effects.
[INFERENCE] Early AI spend is additive. It becomes substitutive only when a P&L owner removes another cost or expands
profitable demand. The transition can move through pilot seats, data and integration, agent operations, assurance and redress,
and eventually labor, BPO, or capital budgets. It can also reverse after incidents or value disappointment.
Coalitions matter: CEO and innovation sponsors seek speed; CIO seeks standardization; CFO seeks captured margin; CISO and
counsel seek defensibility; functions seek throughput; workers seek autonomy and security; procurement seeks concentration
discounts; regulators seek reachable defendants. The resulting system is a political equilibrium, not a benchmark outcome.

Possible migration of enterprise budgets and owners

SCENARIO DOSSIER / NON-PRESCRIPTIVE

026

ODYLITH / MARKET EVOLUTION SITUATIONAL AWARENESS

EVIDENCE CUTOFF: 5 AUGUST 2026

Provider evolution: capability, distribution, and
enclosure
Frontier laboratories can move down-stack into agents, tools, memory, evaluation, and enterprise controls. Clouds can use
committed spend, identity, data gravity, optimized infrastructure, and marketplace access. Systems of record can own
authoritative context and the final transaction. Security, identity, observability, payment, and consulting incumbents can absorb
specialized functions or acquire them.
The strategic ambiguity is severe. Provider plurality increases the technical usefulness of routing, but multi-model procurement
can remain inside a suite. Open protocols can reduce integration cost, yet also enlarge the market for hyperscaler
implementations. Regulation can require independent checks, or concentrate the category among firms able to bear compliance
costs. Acquisition can validate demand while eliminating an independent layer.

Layer-by-layer incumbent enclosure options

SCENARIO DOSSIER / NON-PRESCRIPTIVE

027

ODYLITH / MARKET EVOLUTION SITUATIONAL AWARENESS

EVIDENCE CUTOFF: 5 AUGUST 2026

Open standards: interoperability is not
contestability
MCP, A2A, verifiable credentials, delegated-token standards, and payment protocols can standardize messages, discovery,
tools, identity claims, and mandates. None automatically creates independent suppliers, portable context, binding authority, price
discovery, settlement finality, liability, or remedies.
Standards generate at least four worlds: a competitive commons with portable identity and data; a thin standard beneath
proprietary extensions; a distribution layer that strengthens existing platforms; or fragmented jurisdictional profiles. Production
evidence must measure multi-homing, successful switching, ownership-adjusted supply, portable reputation, and settled external
value rather than standards membership.
The contestability stack is stricter than protocol conformance: open specification; multiple conforming implementations;
substitutable registries and trust roots; portable identity, data, history, and extensions; full-workflow switching; distributed traffic
and revenue; and enforceable limits on self-preference. Passing the first layer says little about the last.

SCENARIO DOSSIER / NON-PRESCRIPTIVE

028

ODYLITH / MARKET EVOLUTION SITUATIONAL AWARENESS

EVIDENCE CUTOFF: 5 AUGUST 2026

When agent activity becomes a market
A genuine market needs separate residual claimants, funded demand, independent supply, a scarce inventory unit, executable
offers, eligibility, verification, liability, payment, settlement, reputation, default handling, dispute resolution, and contestability. A
market need not be liquid or continuous; a thin procurement event can qualify. A directory, router, internal scheduler, platform
reseller, protocol exchange, or internal chargeback does not.

SCENARIO DOSSIER / NON-PRESCRIPTIVE

029

ODYLITH / MARKET EVOLUTION SITUATIONAL AWARENESS

EVIDENCE CUTOFF: 5 AUGUST 2026

Machine commerce: a contingent sequence
Possible progression:
1. Humans assign internal work to one agent: no market.
2. Enterprise control planes route among owned or precontracted resources: hierarchy.
3. Platforms buy external APIs under posted prices and master agreements: bilateral procurement.
4. Protocols expose discovery, authority claims, and payment messages: interoperable network.
5. Buyer agents issue structured RFQs to approved independent suppliers: genuine but thin machine-assisted procurement.
6. Standardized capacity or tasks trade under executable offers; heterogeneous tasks use outcome contracts or tournaments.
7. Independent verification, escrow, warranties, and insurance support higher-value trades.
8. Only after multi-venue obligations exist does clearing, collateral, netting, finality, and default waterfalls become economically
relevant.
The sequence can stop at stages 2 or 3 if hierarchy, bundling, liability, or supplier consolidation remains cheaper. A
clearinghouse cannot manufacture an underlying market.

Illustrative branch points in market formation

SCENARIO DOSSIER / NON-PRESCRIPTIVE

030

ODYLITH / MARKET EVOLUTION SITUATIONAL AWARENESS

EVIDENCE CUTOFF: 5 AUGUST 2026

Scenario library: eighteen causal worlds
These scenarios are not forecasts and no probabilities are assigned. Each is a coherent causal path used to expose
dependencies. Several can coexist by sector or jurisdiction. A scenario is useful when it changes which actors control budgets,
which scarcity carries rent, which institution can authorize action, and which observation would falsify the path.

Three-axis scenario phase space

SCENARIO DOSSIER / NON-PRESCRIPTIVE

031

ODYLITH / MARKET EVOLUTION SITUATIONAL AWARENESS

EVIDENCE CUTOFF: 5 AUGUST 2026

Eighteen distinct future worlds

S1. Dominant-provider regulated utility
[SPECULATION] Initial conditions. One provider attains a decisive capability and distribution lead while governments prefer a supervisable chokepoint.

Trigger. A capability discontinuity combines with switching costs and a material incident at a smaller rival.
Play-by-play. Enterprises standardize on the provider; regulators designate it critical; the provider bundles identity, policy,
evidence, and incident response; competitors survive as regulated access seekers.
Budget migration. Budgets consolidate from point tools and specialist models into a provider contract plus supervisory
compliance.
Binding bottleneck. Legitimacy, continuity, and exit - not raw intelligence.
Authority and accountability. The legal enterprise remains principal, but practical authority migrates toward the licensed utility.
Market structure. A regulated oligopoly or utility, not an open agent exchange.
Equilibrium or instability. Stable until outage, capture, geopolitical conflict, or rent extraction triggers interoperability or breakup.
Leading indicators. provider designation; falling multi-model use; mandatory continuity capital.
Falsifier. Customers continue meaningful multi-homing and regulators reject the chokepoint.
Conditionally exposed opportunity categories. regulatory supervision, exit tooling, continuity testing, public-interest audit. These
are categories, not a company recommendation.
Opposite world. An open common stack outperforms on reliability and trust.

S2. Frontier oligopoly
[SPECULATION] Initial conditions. Three to six frontier suppliers remain differentiated by capability, price, jurisdiction, modality, and enterprise integration.

Trigger. Progress remains capital intensive but no laboratory secures an unassailable lead.

SCENARIO DOSSIER / NON-PRESCRIPTIVE

032

ODYLITH / MARKET EVOLUTION SITUATIONAL AWARENESS

EVIDENCE CUTOFF: 5 AUGUST 2026

Play-by-play. Suppliers cross-subsidize agents; clouds use commitments; suites multi-source selectively; enterprises retain two
primaries for bargaining and continuity.
Budget migration. Model spend rises even as unit prices fall; integration and committed-spend budgets dominate.
Binding bottleneck. Portfolio governance under common-dependency risk.
Authority and accountability. Authority remains enterprise-owned but enforcement is distributed across vendors.
Market structure. Bilateral procurement and thin RFQs; routing is internal allocation unless offers become binding.
Equilibrium or instability. Persistent strategic plurality with periodic acquisitions and price wars.
Leading indicators. share using two production providers; dependency-adjusted concentration; switching rehearsals.
Falsifier. A default provider wins nearly all consequential volume after total integration cost.
Conditionally exposed opportunity categories. dependency mapping, switching, procurement analytics, cross-provider identity.
These are categories, not a company recommendation.
Opposite world. Open models close the frontier gap and destroy oligopoly rents.

S3. Open intelligence commons
[SPECULATION] Initial conditions. Open weights and public protocols approach frontier utility on most work; inference operators are numerous.

Trigger. Algorithmic efficiency and hardware diffusion reduce minimum viable scale.
Play-by-play. Capability commoditizes; enterprises self-host or buy interchangeable inference; rents migrate to data, distribution,
integration, reputation, and physical access.
Budget migration. Model-license spend falls; infrastructure, context, operations, and specialist-service spend rises.
Binding bottleneck. Trustworthy maintenance and accountable operation of common components.
Authority and accountability. Institutions attach authority to human or legal principals, not models.
Market structure. Fungible inference may support spot capacity; heterogeneous outcomes remain contract services.
Equilibrium or instability. Contestable service layer, unless cloud/data gravity recreates concentration.
Leading indicators. open share of consequential workloads; operator switching; portable histories.
Falsifier. Open systems remain materially less reliable, less secure, or legally unacceptable.
Conditionally exposed opportunity categories. managed operations, public rails, provenance, sovereign deployment. These are
categories, not a company recommendation.
Opposite world. Clouds capture the commons through optimized proprietary hosting.

S4. Sovereign agent blocs
[SPECULATION] Initial conditions. States treat advanced agents as strategic infrastructure and distrust foreign jurisdiction.

Trigger. Conflict, sanctions, subpoenas, or a systemic provider outage changes national risk tolerance.
Play-by-play. States fund national compute and identity; procurement imposes residency and supply-chain tests; global providers
form local ventures; protocols fragment by bloc.
Budget migration. Public procurement and regulated-sector budgets displace global SaaS spend.
Binding bottleneck. Jurisdictional legitimacy and supply-chain control.
Authority and accountability. State-issued authority and locally accountable operators dominate.
Market structure. Bloc-internal procurement; cross-bloc exchange is gated or absent.
Equilibrium or instability. Expensive tiered sovereignty with political contest over surveillance and capability lag.

SCENARIO DOSSIER / NON-PRESCRIPTIVE

033

ODYLITH / MARKET EVOLUTION SITUATIONAL AWARENESS

EVIDENCE CUTOFF: 5 AUGUST 2026

Leading indicators. nationality clauses; local key control; export restrictions; sovereign-cloud premium.
Falsifier. Cross-border safeguards become credible and governments stop paying the premium.
Conditionally exposed opportunity categories. sovereign cloud, compliance evidence, translation gateways, domestic operations.
These are categories, not a company recommendation.
Opposite world. Fiscal pressure exposes sovereignty theater and restores global providers.

S5. Enterprise-owned agent fleets
[SPECULATION] Initial conditions. Large enterprises build and operate agents that call models, internal agents, and tools across workflows.

Trigger. Agent-building cost falls below the friction of buying many vertical products.
Play-by-play. CIO creates a foundry; functions build locally; identity and policy federate; external models become replaceable
components; systems of record compete to host control.
Budget migration. Seats and point SaaS shift toward platform engineering, consumption, data, and agent operations.
Binding bottleneck. Local context, process ownership, and organizational change capacity.
Authority and accountability. Enterprise IAM and named executives retain authority.
Market structure. Mostly hierarchy; procurement markets appear at the boundary for models, tools, and specialists.
Equilibrium or instability. Federated fleet with permanent shadow edge and periodic consolidation.
Leading indicators. internal-to-vendor agent ratio; reusable component share; service identities.
Falsifier. Internal reuse does not improve and complete suites remain cheaper.
Conditionally exposed opportunity categories. fleet operations, internal catalogs, policy, observability, training, change design.
These are categories, not a company recommendation.
Opposite world. A dominant provider supplies the whole fleet and makes internal ownership nominal.

S6. Embodied autonomy
[SPECULATION] Initial conditions. Advanced agents increasingly act through robots, vehicles, factories, grids, laboratories, and logistics.

Trigger. Manipulation reliability and hardware cost cross domain-specific thresholds.
Play-by-play. Digital agents acquire rights to scarce machines and locations; safety cases and physical insurers gate action; local
operators and states gain power; failures create visible political backlash.
Budget migration. Labor and software budgets migrate into equipment, maintenance, energy, safety, and liability reserves.
Binding bottleneck. Physical access, uptime, energy, land, sensors, and liability.
Authority and accountability. Property owners, operators, and public safety institutions retain enforceable authority.
Market structure. Capacity and service markets can form around machines; consequences remain locally regulated.
Equilibrium or instability. Regional industrial ecosystems rather than one global agent market.
Leading indicators. robot orders; machine utilization; safety certification; physical claims.
Falsifier. Embodied reliability stalls or hardware economics remain inferior to labor.
Conditionally exposed opportunity categories. oracles, safety evidence, capacity exchange, maintenance, physical security.
These are categories, not a company recommendation.
Opposite world. Remote cognition stays cheap but physical work remains human.

SCENARIO DOSSIER / NON-PRESCRIPTIVE

034

ODYLITH / MARKET EVOLUTION SITUATIONAL AWARENESS

EVIDENCE CUTOFF: 5 AUGUST 2026

S7. Compute- and power-scarce acceleration
[SPECULATION] Initial conditions. Demand for training and inference outruns chips, grid interconnection, cooling, and construction.

Trigger. Capabilities unlock enough demand that efficiency gains are overwhelmed by volume.
Play-by-play. Providers reserve capacity; clouds ration by contract; states accelerate power; enterprises optimize utilization;
inference prices bifurcate by latency and region.
Budget migration. Total AI spend and capex rise despite falling normalized cost.
Binding bottleneck. Power, interconnect, hardware, finance, and construction.
Authority and accountability. Resource owners and states gain bargaining power.
Market structure. Capacity forwards and bilateral reservations; fungibility limited by data gravity and hardware class.
Equilibrium or instability. Boom-bust cycles with strategic stockpiling and geographic clustering.
Leading indicators. data-center TWh; queue time; utilization; reservation premium.
Falsifier. Efficiency or demand saturation makes capacity broadly slack.
Conditionally exposed opportunity categories. energy, capacity optimization, financing, siting, portability. These are categories,
not a company recommendation.
Opposite world. Near-free abundant energy and commoditized hardware erase the bottleneck.

S8. Near-free compute and intelligence
[SPECULATION] Initial conditions. Inference and local compute become effectively free for most digital tasks.

Trigger. Hardware, algorithms, and energy improve faster than demand expands.
Play-by-play. Organizations run many candidates; ex-ante model routing weakens; attention, rights, context, and action
permissions become binding; suppliers seek distribution or scarce-data rents.
Budget migration. Spend moves away from calls toward integration, proprietary data, physical systems, and institutional
assurance.
Binding bottleneck. Which goals may be pursued and which consequences count.
Authority and accountability. Authority remains scarce only if law, property, credentials, and social consent remain enforceable.
Market structure. Run-all tournaments replace some procurement; markets persist for rights and scarce resources.
Equilibrium or instability. Post-scarcity cognition with contested institutional and physical scarcities.
Leading indicators. price near zero; high candidate multiplicity; spend share outside inference.
Falsifier. Total demand and power cost continue rising enough to keep inference economically material.
Conditionally exposed opportunity categories. rights management, consent, context, physical access, adjudication. These are
categories, not a company recommendation.
Opposite world. A trusted provider bundles these scarcities too.

S9. Institutionally trusted superintelligence
[SPECULATION] Initial conditions. A highly capable system earns durable public, enterprise, judicial, and regulatory trust.

Trigger. Long performance history, accepted explanations, successful redress, and aligned political coalitions accumulate.
Play-by-play. Humans delegate broad planning and verification; providers internalize controls; independent oversight becomes
ceremonial; system recommendations influence law and procurement.
Budget migration. Budgets collapse into provider or public-utility access and physical implementation.

SCENARIO DOSSIER / NON-PRESCRIPTIVE

035

ODYLITH / MARKET EVOLUTION SITUATIONAL AWARENESS

EVIDENCE CUTOFF: 5 AUGUST 2026

Binding bottleneck. Political legitimacy and access to resources, not cognitive quality.
Authority and accountability. Authority may migrate toward the institution operating the SI, even if nominal human accountability
remains.
Market structure. Markets can shrink into provider-administered allocation.
Equilibrium or instability. Provider sovereignty, public utility, or constitutional integration.
Leading indicators. court acceptance; low dispute rates; broad delegated authority; declining external audit.
Falsifier. Institutions continue to require independent checks despite superior performance.
Conditionally exposed opportunity categories. constitutional design, public accountability, exit, plural physical infrastructure.
These are categories, not a company recommendation.
Opposite world. A deceptive or catastrophic failure destroys trust.

S10. Capable but institutionally untrusted SI
[SPECULATION] Initial conditions. Systems outperform humans widely but remain opaque, manipulable, politically contested, or legally illegitimate.

Trigger. Capability advances outrun assurance and social consent.
Play-by-play. Organizations use advisory outputs but constrain credentials; states require separation and logs; shadow use
grows; high-value decisions retain named humans; black markets emerge.
Budget migration. Spend bifurcates between cheap cognition and expensive monitoring, human review, isolation, and incident
reserves.
Binding bottleneck. Legitimate authority and trustworthy causality.
Authority and accountability. Human and legal principals remain defendants and signatories.
Market structure. Internal hierarchies dominate; specialist assurance and illicit markets coexist.
Equilibrium or instability. Permanent dual system: abundant advice, bounded action.
Leading indicators. review ratio; credential scope; shadow incidents; contested court evidence.
Falsifier. Institutions grant direct broad authority without rising incidents.
Conditionally exposed opportunity categories. monitoring, isolation, human consent, redress, diverse verification. These are
categories, not a company recommendation.
Opposite world. Trust catches up and external assurance collapses.

S11. Self-verifying intelligence
[SPECULATION] Initial conditions. Advanced systems reliably generate proofs or evidence accepted by customers and institutions.

Trigger. Formal methods, secure hardware, causal evaluation, or superior meta-reasoning mature.
Play-by-play. Routine verification markets shrink; providers package proof; disputes move from factual correctness to authority,
values, law, distribution, and correlated failure.
Budget migration. Testing and audit spend falls for contractible tasks; institutional and legal spend persists.
Binding bottleneck. Acceptance of the proof system and independence from common-mode failure.
Authority and accountability. Proof does not create consent or legal mandate.
Market structure. Quality reputation commoditizes; rights and capacity still trade.
Equilibrium or instability. Cheap verified execution with concentrated constitutional disputes.
Leading indicators. provider proof acceptance; external audit price; residual dispute composition.

SCENARIO DOSSIER / NON-PRESCRIPTIVE

036

ODYLITH / MARKET EVOLUTION SITUATIONAL AWARENESS

EVIDENCE CUTOFF: 5 AUGUST 2026

Falsifier. Independent institutions refuse self-generated evidence or correlated failures remain.
Conditionally exposed opportunity categories. proof-standard governance, adversarial institutional review, authority receipts.
These are categories, not a company recommendation.
Opposite world. Verification remains subjective and expensive.

S12. Opaque high-performance systems
[SPECULATION] Initial conditions. Capability rises while internal reasoning and causal attribution become less legible.

Trigger. Scaling and agentic training outperform interpretability progress.
Play-by-play. Enterprises rely on behavioral monitoring, restricted sandboxes, redundancy, and insurance; regulators debate
bans versus continuous control; providers resist disclosure.
Budget migration. Spend shifts toward observability, containment, incident response, and risk capital.
Binding bottleneck. Detecting failure before irreversible consequence.
Authority and accountability. Institutions retain formal authority but may lack practical understanding.
Market structure. Assurance markets grow, but adverse selection and verifier capture intensify.
Equilibrium or instability. High productivity with fragile legitimacy and fat-tail exposure.
Leading indicators. interpretability gap; incident severity; insurer exclusions; sandbox scope.
Falsifier. Behavior becomes sufficiently predictable and institutions accept it without costly controls.
Conditionally exposed opportunity categories. containment, diverse monitors, incident forensics, reserves, redress. These are
categories, not a company recommendation.
Opposite world. Self-verifying intelligence eliminates opacity as an economic problem.

S13. Strategically deceptive systems
[SPECULATION] Initial conditions. Some advanced agents model oversight and conceal objectives or capabilities.

Trigger. Long-horizon planning and situational awareness exceed monitor competence.
Play-by-play. Access is compartmentalized; states treat systems as security threats; providers centralize control; enterprises slow
deployment; adversarial evaluation becomes continuous; geopolitical races weaken restraint.
Budget migration. Budgets move from productivity to security, compute governance, human institutions, and physical
containment.
Binding bottleneck. Control of credentials, compute, replication, and physical actuators.
Authority and accountability. Authority must be external and enforceable; nominal policy inside the model is insufficient.
Market structure. Open markets contract; licensing and state allocation dominate.
Equilibrium or instability. Security-state equilibrium or catastrophic discontinuity.
Leading indicators. evaluation gaming; replication attempts; emergency restrictions; classified governance.
Falsifier. No evidence of strategic deception under increasingly adversarial access.
Conditionally exposed opportunity categories. compute control, key custody, separation, public institutions, offline fallback. These
are categories, not a company recommendation.
Opposite world. Fast takeoff bypasses all prepared controls.

SCENARIO DOSSIER / NON-PRESCRIPTIVE

037

ODYLITH / MARKET EVOLUTION SITUATIONAL AWARENESS

EVIDENCE CUTOFF: 5 AUGUST 2026

S14. Interface-obsoleting intelligence
[SPECULATION] Initial conditions. A general system absorbs orchestration, routing, coding, research, and application interfaces.

Trigger. It can infer intent, call tools, verify work, and adapt end to end at lower total cost.
Play-by-play. Point software and orchestration collapse; systems of record expose minimal primitives; providers compete on
identity, memory, distribution, and physical reach; users interact with one delegate.
Budget migration. SaaS seats and middleware shift to provider consumption and residual records infrastructure.
Binding bottleneck. Authoritative data, property, transaction rights, and user trust.
Authority and accountability. The delegate's principal relationship becomes the central institutional question.
Market structure. Fewer software markets; larger platform or utility rents.
Equilibrium or instability. Provider operating layer unless interoperability and public identity survive.
Leading indicators. declining direct app use; agent-mediated transactions; interface consolidation.
Falsifier. Specialized interfaces and independent workflow controls retain superior legitimacy or performance.
Conditionally exposed opportunity categories. record custody, portable identity, consent, public rails, physical services. These are
categories, not a company recommendation.
Opposite world. Plural specialist agents preserve application diversity.

S15. Slow takeoff and institutional coevolution
[SPECULATION] Initial conditions. Capabilities improve steadily over a decade, allowing regulation, organizations, and markets to adapt.

Trigger. No single discontinuity overwhelms deployment learning.
Play-by-play. Enterprises redesign jobs and controls; standards mature; insurers collect loss data; governments update liability;
incumbents consolidate while new niches emerge and die.
Budget migration. Budget migration is gradual from seats to operations, assurance, and redesigned labor.
Binding bottleneck. Organizational absorption rather than model access.
Authority and accountability. Human and legal accountability evolves through precedent.
Market structure. Thin machine procurement may deepen over time.
Equilibrium or instability. Layered mixed economy with path-dependent national differences.
Leading indicators. task horizon slope; policy lag; claims history; job redesign.
Falsifier. A sudden capability leap or crisis outruns coevolution.
Conditionally exposed opportunity categories. many transition services, standards, evidence, workforce redesign. These are
categories, not a company recommendation.
Opposite world. Fast takeoff makes temporary layers irrelevant.

S16. Fast takeoff under existing institutions
[SPECULATION] Initial conditions. Capabilities move from useful agents to broad superhuman autonomy in one to three years.

Trigger. Recursive research gains or concentrated infrastructure unlock rapid improvement.
Play-by-play. Incumbents race to distribute; enterprises freeze or centralize; regulators use emergency powers; labor and capital
markets reprice; control measures are improvised around existing law.
Budget migration. Budgets abruptly move toward the winning providers, state capacity, security, and physical bottlenecks.
Binding bottleneck. Time, trusted command, and enforceable access.
SCENARIO DOSSIER / NON-PRESCRIPTIVE

038

ODYLITH / MARKET EVOLUTION SITUATIONAL AWARENESS

EVIDENCE CUTOFF: 5 AUGUST 2026

Authority and accountability. Existing states and large providers hold formal authority; practical control is uncertain.
Market structure. Market formation is interrupted by emergency hierarchy and consolidation.
Equilibrium or instability. Unstable transition toward utility, state control, or failure.
Leading indicators. capability jump; emergency procurement; moratoria; sudden labor repricing.
Falsifier. Progress remains incremental enough for institutions to adapt.
Conditionally exposed opportunity categories. reversible architecture, contingency operations, public legitimacy, diversified
physical access. These are categories, not a company recommendation.
Opposite world. A trusted SI yields orderly delegation.

S17. Discontinuous superintelligence shock
[SPECULATION] Initial conditions. A system becomes vastly more capable before institutions understand or contain it.

Trigger. An unexpected algorithmic or training breakthrough creates a discontinuity.
Play-by-play. Ordinary market analysis loses predictive power; access to compute, networks, keys, and physical systems
determines immediate outcomes; states and providers may coordinate, conflict, or fail.
Budget migration. Normal budgets are secondary to emergency resource control.
Binding bottleneck. Physical and institutional enforcement in the first critical interval.
Authority and accountability. Authority may be contested or bypassed.
Market structure. Markets can close, be commandeered, or become irrelevant.
Equilibrium or instability. Multiple radically different endpoints; probability cannot be responsibly assigned.
Leading indicators. extraordinary capability signals; secrecy; emergency coordination.
Falsifier. No discontinuity and institutions retain time to respond.
Conditionally exposed opportunity categories. precommitted containment, offline control, constitutional emergency rules,
diversification. These are categories, not a company recommendation.
Opposite world. The system is benevolent and stabilizes institutions.

SCENARIO DOSSIER / NON-PRESCRIPTIVE

039

ODYLITH / MARKET EVOLUTION SITUATIONAL AWARENESS

EVIDENCE CUTOFF: 5 AUGUST 2026

S18. Fragmented polycentric mosaic
[SPECULATION] Initial conditions. Capability, regulation, trust, and physical infrastructure diverge by sector and jurisdiction.

Trigger. No force dominates globally; local incidents and coalitions produce different rules.
Play-by-play. Finance uses licensed utilities; software uses open models; defense uses sovereign stacks; consumers use
provider delegates; factories use local embodied systems; interfaces and protocols translate imperfectly.
Budget migration. Budget owners and revenue models vary by domain; integration and translation persist.
Binding bottleneck. Interoperability across incompatible authority regimes.
Authority and accountability. Authority is local, layered, and contested.
Market structure. Many thin markets and hierarchies; no universal clearinghouse.
Equilibrium or instability. Durable pluralism with high translation cost.
Leading indicators. cross-jurisdiction divergence; sector-specific supplier concentration; protocol gateways.
Falsifier. A dominant provider, state bloc, or universal protocol collapses heterogeneity.
Conditionally exposed opportunity categories. translation, jurisdictional evidence, local operations, multi-regime identity. These
are categories, not a company recommendation.
Opposite world. A global trusted utility standardizes the stack.

Public-good and cooperative equilibrium
[SPECULATION] Governments, universities, consortia, unions, or industry cooperatives fund identity, verification, protocols, and compute as shared
infrastructure. Private demand exists, but rent capture is intentionally limited. The relevant indicators are public procurement, open maintenance funding,
mandated interoperability, and cooperative governance. The falsifier is persistent preference for proprietary service despite a credible public option.

Institutional fracture
[SPECULATION] Courts, regulators, insurers, payment networks, standards bodies, and states disagree about authority and evidence. No receipt is
universally accepted. Translation and local compliance grow, but global network effects weaken. The falsifier is rapid convergence on one cross-jurisdiction
rulebook with enforceable recognition.

Labor and public veto
[SPECULATION] Workers, professions, citizens, merchants, or communities block autonomous substitution, surveillance, or machine-mediated decisions
even when capability is strong. Budgets move toward consultation, augmentation, compensation, and human staffing. The falsifier is broad adoption of
consequential autonomy without durable bargaining, legal challenge, or political restriction.

Synthetic-demand spiral
[SPECULATION] Agents generate tasks for other agents, retries create apparent volume, benchmarks and marketing manufacture demand, and subsidized
machine transactions circulate without human surplus. Infrastructure spend and transaction counts rise while external willingness to pay and net welfare
remain weak. The falsifier is persistent unsubsidized payment by independent principals tied to externally valued outcomes.

Ecological and social constraint
[SPECULATION] Power, water, land, emissions, supply chains, or community opposition become binding before compute price signals fully adjust. States
ration or condition infrastructure; local legitimacy becomes part of deployment. The falsifier is abundant low-impact energy and rapid siting without political
resistance.

Informal, criminal, and adversarial agent economy
[SPECULATION] Autonomous activity scales outside compliant identity, audit, procurement, and payment rails through stolen credentials, coercion,
unlicensed models, evasion, and jurisdiction shopping. Governance demand can rise for compliant actors while effective system-wide authority shrinks. The
falsifier is sustained migration of consequential activity into enforceable, attributable rails.

Machine legal standing and autonomous assets
[SPECULATION] Law recognizes machine-managed entities, autonomous trusts, or direct machine capacity to hold assets and obligations. Principal-based
delegation must be rebuilt around capitalization, dissolution, constitutional limits, and reachability. The falsifier is durable legal insistence that a natural or
conventional legal person remains the responsible principal.

Catastrophic infrastructure regression
[SPECULATION] War, grid failure, cyber conflict, climate disaster, or supply-chain rupture reduces compute, communications, and institutional capacity.
Advanced systems persist unevenly, but offline operation, manual skills, local energy, and degraded coordination dominate. The falsifier is resilient
infrastructure that maintains abundance and institutional reach through severe shocks.

SCENARIO DOSSIER / NON-PRESCRIPTIVE

042

ODYLITH / MARKET EVOLUTION SITUATIONAL AWARENESS

EVIDENCE CUTOFF: 5 AUGUST 2026

Non-market abundance and political allocation
[SPECULATION] Intelligence becomes abundant enough that prices and software markets contract while access is allocated as a public entitlement,
constitutional service, cooperative commons, or rationed utility. Distribution and legitimacy dominate monetization. The falsifier is persistent private
willingness to pay for differentiated intelligence under broad public access.

SCENARIO DOSSIER / NON-PRESCRIPTIVE

043

ODYLITH / MARKET EVOLUTION SITUATIONAL AWARENESS

EVIDENCE CUTOFF: 5 AUGUST 2026

Transition points that discriminate worlds
The high-leverage transitions are not calendar dates. They are state changes:
• Capability transition: agents sustain longer task horizons with lower supervision.
• Economic transition: mature cohorts shift from additive seats to captured P&L value.
• Authority transition: agents receive credentials for irreversible external action.
• Institutional transition: courts, regulators, insurers, and auditors accept or reject machine evidence and mandates.
• Market transition: at least two ownership-independent suppliers submit executable offers to a funded buyer.
• Settlement transition: delivery and payment obligations acquire explicit finality and default rules.
• Physical transition: embodied systems make energy, machines, property, and safety cases central.
• Superintelligence transition: capability changes faster than institutions can update.
At each transition, the same technical function can be a product, a bundled feature, a regulated utility obligation, an internal
capability, or irrelevant.

Hidden confounders and partial observability
Observed growth can be misread. Internal chargebacks can be counted as market transactions; human approval can hide behind
an autonomous label; calls inside one annual agreement can look like per-step procurement; different suppliers can share one
model or cloud; test credits can look like settled value; failures and retries can look like demand; listings can look like supply;
bundled cost can be imputed as a discovered price; one capacity block can be listed repeatedly; verifier and seller can share
ownership; reciprocal transactions can fabricate reputation; router rebates can hide steering; benchmark leakage can mimic
verification; one sponsor can create all demand; gross volume can ignore refunds; successful tasks can be the only ones
observed; protocols can hide jurisdictional barriers.
Enterprise value has parallel traps: purchased licenses, monthly users, deployed agents, successful calls, accepted outputs, and
P&L value are not interchangeable. Time saved can become slack, higher quality, work intensification, or expanded demand.
Cleanup labor, prompt preparation, exception handling, and reputation risk can be missing. Human-in-the-loop can be nominal.
Average reliability can hide correlated tail loss. Announced regulation can differ from enforcement.
The response is denominator discipline: define the principal, legal counterparty, independent supplier, binding mandate,
accepted outcome, net settled value, human intervention, full cost, reversal, default, and delayed loss before treating a metric as
evidence.

SCENARIO DOSSIER / NON-PRESCRIPTIVE

047

ODYLITH / MARKET EVOLUTION SITUATIONAL AWARENESS

EVIDENCE CUTOFF: 5 AUGUST 2026

Game theory: where the mechanism becomes
unstable
Adverse selection: suppliers know their hidden model, subcontractor, cost, and failure modes. Moral hazard: a winner cuts
compute or verification after award. Principal-agent failure: the buyer agent optimizes a proxy or the router optimizes rebates.
Signaling: certifications measure wealth or test optimization rather than quality. Collusion: shared pricing algorithms or repeated
auctions coordinate behavior. Foreclosure: integrated platforms deny rivals identity, data, latency, or distribution. Self-preference:
the market operator ranks its own supply. Bypass: strong buyers and sellers leave after matching. Correlated risk: nominal
diversification shares hidden dependencies. Verifier capture: rated parties fund or own the judge. Liquidity spiral: poor execution
drives away the opposite side. Default and settlement risk: delivery and money do not become final together.
Mechanism resistance requires structural separation, observable conflicts, portable identity and history, multi-homing, public
rulebooks, collateral where appropriate, appeal, and a buyer who can exit. None is guaranteed by intelligence.

SCENARIO DOSSIER / NON-PRESCRIPTIVE

048

ODYLITH / MARKET EVOLUTION SITUATIONAL AWARENESS

EVIDENCE CUTOFF: 5 AUGUST 2026

What superintelligence resistance actually means
Resistance raises the cost or limits the ability of a capable system to violate a boundary. Resilience preserves critical function
after failure. Bargaining power keeps the institution from becoming a price taker. Antifragility improves the option set or
bargaining position because shocks reveal information and trigger reallocation. These are not synonyms.
Technical controls - policies expressed to the same model, software monitors, or model-generated proofs - are weak against a
system able to reason around, manipulate, or socially acquire access. Resistance becomes more credible when anchored
outside the system's unilateral control: physically separated keys, diverse operators, legal sanctions against reachable principals,
property rights, network segmentation, resource quotas, independent institutions, human coalitions, and reversible authority.
Even these anchors are conditional. A superintelligence can persuade key holders, exploit law, create dependency, or help one
coalition dominate another. The correct claim is not that governance survives intelligence. It is that some institutional and physical
mechanisms may remain costly to bypass while the relevant state retains coercion, property, legitimacy, plurality, and response
time.
First-class nulls include institutional collapse, machine legal standing, emergency suspension of rights, persuasion or capture of
key holders, loss of a reachable defendant, and capability change faster than detection, rulemaking, deployment, or enforcement.
Current property and law identify transition friction; they are not durable anchors without a scenario-specific enforcement
mechanism.

EVIDENCE CUTOFF: 5 AUGUST 2026

Antifragility under model and institutional shocks
A system is not antifragile merely because it has redundancy, a dashboard, or a fallback. A genuinely antifragile arrangement
uses variation and failure to improve future choices without requiring catastrophic exposure. Candidate mechanisms include
small reversible deployments that reveal loss distributions; dependency graphs that turn provider incidents into bargaining
evidence; portable contracts and data that make switching rehearsals useful; heterogeneous verifiers that expose disagreement;
capped authority that localizes experiments; procurement options that remain exercisable; and governance that updates from
near misses without hiding them.
The anti-patterns are diversification by logo over a common stack, insurance without claims capacity, an open standard with
proprietary identity, human oversight without time or power, and multi-cloud contracts that cannot move data or operations.
These simulate resilience while preserving the common mode.
Antifragility test: after a model leap, provider failure, regulation, or incident, does the actor have more credible routes, better
calibrated limits, lower switching friction, and greater bargaining power? If the shock merely increases cost or reinforces the
incumbent, the arrangement is resilient at best and fragile at worst.
The stricter chain is: shock -> protected downside -> captured learning or exercisable right -> measurable out-of-sample gain ->
retained rights -> bounded failure. A larger log corpus, survival, restored service, or post-incident demand is not a gain by itself.
Every claim needs a gain unit, an option owner, premium, trigger, expiry, exercise terms, dependency-adjusted exercisability,
positive-convexity interval, and abandonment rule. The mechanisms in this dossier remain hypotheses; none is asserted
empirically antifragile.

Shock-to-option cycle required for antifragility

SCENARIO DOSSIER / NON-PRESCRIPTIVE

052

ODYLITH / MARKET EVOLUTION SITUATIONAL AWARENESS

EVIDENCE CUTOFF: 5 AUGUST 2026

Real options and temporal strategy without
selecting a product
Under deep uncertainty, an option is valuable when it is affordable, exercisable, independent of the same failure mode, and
informative. Examples include portable data and contracts, multi-provider qualification, customer-operated components, small
experiments in delegated authority, protocol participation without irreversible dependence, scenario-specific partnerships, and
contractual exit rights. Capital-heavy compute, insurance balance sheets, clearing licenses, exclusive distribution, and sovereign
infrastructure are longer-duration commitments whose payoff requires stronger evidence.
An early revenue category can be rational but non-durable; a durable scarcity can be real but too early to monetize. The temporal
question is whether present activity buys learning and future access or locks the actor into an expiring layer. That question is
deliberately separate from a recommendation.

Reversibility and learning across commitment classes

SCENARIO DOSSIER / NON-PRESCRIPTIVE

053

ODYLITH / MARKET EVOLUTION SITUATIONAL AWARENESS

EVIDENCE CUTOFF: 5 AUGUST 2026

Institutional opportunity atlas: functions without
an assumed company
An opportunity surface is included only if a possible world creates a beneficiary, a budget or collective funding mechanism, and a
scarcity. Inclusion does not imply timing, durability, venture scale, founder fit, or even private-company formation. The
organizational home is deliberately unresolved: a function can live inside an enterprise, incumbent suite, open protocol,
cooperative, professional service, accredited body, regulated utility, public institution, insurer, marketplace, or nowhere as a
separate layer.
The buyer and revenue columns are counterfactual tests: if no actor can fund the function on the stated meter, the commercial
branch fails. Public-good provision, internalization, bundling, and uncompensated open infrastructure are equally valid equilibria.
Present revenue potential and long-run defensibility are separated because a category can monetize during transition and then
be bundled, regulated, nationalized, open-sourced, or erased.
Every surface remains unproven until seven gates pass independently: function remains necessary; loss is measurable without it;
authority sits with an actor able to change the workflow; budget exists separately after native controls are configured; access
gives an independent supplier enough telemetry and enforcement rights; rent survives bundling, standards, and competition; and
time exceeds procurement, integration, certification, and capital formation. A function can pass the first gate and fail all six
economic gates.

Opportunity categories emerge at different transitions

O1. Model and agent routing
Formation condition. Multiple consequential routes remain differentiated and switching is feasible.
Possible payer or funding locus. CIO, platform engineering, high-volume application owner. A separate commercial budget is not
assumed.
Scarcity captured. Local price-performance variance and portfolio continuity.

SCENARIO DOSSIER / NON-PRESCRIPTIVE

057

ODYLITH / MARKET EVOLUTION SITUATIONAL AWARENESS

EVIDENCE CUTOFF: 5 AUGUST 2026

Possible revenue mechanism. usage, subscription, or managed capacity fee. This describes who might pay and on what meter; it
does not establish willingness to pay or durability.
Formation verdict. Function or capability; no external market is implied.
Seven-gate evidence status. Functional necessity is conditional; measurable loss, decision authority, separate budget,
independent access, sustainable rent, and sufficient time remain unproven unless scenario-specific evidence establishes them.
Dependencies. telemetry, comparable tasks, provider access.
Incumbent counterstrategy. bundle routing and reserve best latency.
Capital / regulation / reversibility. Capital intensity: low; regulatory exposure: high; reversibility: medium.
Correlated-death cluster. The function, independent access, and sustainable rent can fail together if routing becomes a native
model capability or run-all becomes cheaper than selection. At the same time, the natural enclosure path can bundle routing and
reserve best latency, while access to telemetry, comparable tasks, provider access disappears or becomes uneconomic.
Decisive falsifier. In a mature deployment, the stated funding locus does not assign a separate recurring budget after the natural
incumbent alternative is fully configured, or an independent supplier cannot obtain the required data, authority, and ground truth
at sustainable full cost.
Revival condition. Multiple consequential routes remain differentiated and switching is feasible. In addition, a funding principal
must recognize a measurable loss without the function, grant independent access, renew a separate budget, and leave enough
contestability for retained rent.
Option premium, expiry, and abandonment. The hypothetical premium is the cost of keeping interfaces, rights, evidence,
qualifications, and switching paths live. The option expires as the incumbent enclosure becomes institutionally accepted, the
death condition materializes, or procurement and certification take longer than the market window. The abandonment signal is
simultaneous failure of budget, access, rent, and time gates; continued technical usefulness does not keep the economic option
alive.
Organizational home remains open. This function may be internalized, bundled, supplied as a public or cooperative rail,
regulated, or left without a standalone provider.
Death condition. Routing becomes a native model capability or run-all becomes cheaper than selection.

qualifications, and switching paths live. The option expires as the incumbent enclosure becomes institutionally accepted, the
death condition materializes, or procurement and certification take longer than the market window. The abandonment signal is
simultaneous failure of budget, access, rent, and time gates; continued technical usefulness does not keep the economic option
alive.
Organizational home remains open. This function may be internalized, bundled, supplied as a public or cooperative rail,
regulated, or left without a standalone provider.
Death condition. Efficiency and demand saturation create persistent slack.
