Round 3 independent adjudication - historical
pre-closure critique
Status note. This adjudication is preserved verbatim as the pre-closure critique. The integrated source answers its fourteen
required corrections in the Round 3 correction-closure ledger; the critique below is not a claim that those defects remain open.
Date: 2026-08-05

Scope and adjudicative standard
This adjudication reviews Odylith_Market_Evolution_Situational_Awareness.md and all six Round 2 records:
enterprise authority, incumbents, market economics, mechanism design, superintelligence resistance, and antifragility,
falsification, and real options. The last record is treated as part of the final evidence set, including its existential kill tree, A01-A25
falsification register, M01-M20 mechanism audit, C01-C22 kill and revival cards, and option-decay tests.
The standard here is non-prescriptive. The question is not what Odylith should build, whether a company should exist, or whether
an investor should act. It is whether the dossier has produced a sufficiently unanchored and causally discriminating world model.
The tests are: internal consistency; independence from the originating thesis; separation of function, institution, market, and
company; explicit treatment of null worlds; evidence proportionality; and conditional rather than rhetorical opportunity claims.
Source locators below name exact dossier headings and, where useful, exact claims. Round 2 locators name the corresponding
memo and debate. These citations are internal evidence references, not endorsements of the external sources cited inside those
records.

Executive determination
The dossier is materially stronger than the prior strategy frame it criticizes. It explicitly states in # Mandate: build a world
model before choosing a strategy that it does not assume a router, control plane, marketplace, clearinghouse,
verification layer, governance company, software beachhead, or outcome business must exist. It correctly says in # Present
topology: hierarchy before market that internal allocation is hierarchy and in # Open standards:
interoperability is not contestability that message compatibility does not establish independent supply, portable
authority, settlement, or remedies. The Round 2 mechanism memo supplies the strongest discipline in the corpus: independent
principals, funded demand, executable allocation, liability, payment, settlement, default, remedies, and contestability are required
before market language is earned.
Those are real corrections, not cosmetic ones. The dossier also distinguishes marginal inference cost from total system
expenditure, nominal provider plurality from customer-control concentration, technical verification from institutional acceptance,
resilience from antifragility, and a persistent function from a separately monetizable company. Its superintelligence worlds include
genuine death conditions for routing, external assurance, human review, and rich control layers.
The adverse finding is that the synthesis has not fully escaped the originating frame. Its disclaimers are less anchored than its
taxonomy. The title is still Odylith Market Evolution Situational Awareness; the first ten entries of the #
Opportunity atlas: conditional surfaces, not selections map closely onto an agent control-plane
decomposition; and many later entries are the institutional complements that such a decomposition would need. The document
says the deck is only a hypothesis generator, but the generated opportunity inventory still resembles a broadened perimeter
around that deck. This is not proof that any category is wrong.
The antifragility memo supplies useful counterevidence to a claim of simple deck copying: its ## Post-hoc collision with
the supplied deck says its analysis was derived before reviewing the deck, and its independently generated C01-C22
functions overlap materially with the dossier. That overlap raises confidence that several functions are real analytical objects. It
does not prove complete frame independence because the memo's starting scope already asks whether an independent
economic position can exist around routing, context, verification, authorization, governance, evidence, reputation, procurement,
settlement, insurance, and trust. It is blind to the deck but not blind to an Odylith-adjacent problem statement. A genuinely
independent second taxonomy must begin from actors, welfare, loss allocation, public goods, labor, households, coercion, and
SCENARIO DOSSIER / NON-PRESCRIPTIVE

164

ODYLITH / MARKET EVOLUTION SITUATIONAL AWARENESS

EVIDENCE CUTOFF: 5 AUGUST 2026

non-market institutions rather than from candidate trust-infrastructure functions.
The second adverse finding is that governance and authority receive many death-world qualifications but remain the dossier's
default residual scarcities. The synthesis repeatedly moves value from cognition to context, credentials, proof, authority,
legitimacy, physical resources, or consent. It does acknowledge that each can die, yet it does not model enough worlds in which
enforceable governance is absent, irrelevant, captured, non-commercial, or unable to keep pace. The phrase in # What
superintelligence resistance actually means that some mechanisms may remain costly to bypass is properly
conditional; the opportunity atlas often turns the same conditional functions back into named economic surfaces before separate
budget, supplier access, and rent durability have been shown.
The third finding is more favorable: the dossier generally does not confuse hierarchy with market in its definitions, but it
sometimes lets market-shaped opportunity language leak back into scenarios where only procurement, internal allocation, or a
platform supply chain exists. The mechanism memo corrects this more rigorously than the main synthesis. The main dossier
should import that stricter vocabulary everywhere, not only in # When agent activity becomes a market.
The fourth finding is that the dossier explicitly rejects standards-as-contestability, but it has not fully operationalized the rejection.
O24. Protocol infrastructure still assigns scarcity to "Common syntax and network effects." Common syntax may be a
public good with no appropriable rent, and network effects can accrue to the dominant runtime, registry, identity root, or
distribution platform rather than to protocol infrastructure. The correction must distinguish interoperability, portability,
multi-homing, demand control, governance control, and economic contestability as separate variables.

Agreements that survive cross-examination
1. Cheap intelligence and concentrated control can coexist
All six Round 2 records support the dossier's rejection of a one-dimensional abundance story. The market-economics memo
shows that falling accepted-task cost can coexist with frontier fixed costs, committed spend, data gravity, and platform bundling.
The incumbent memo shows that open or cheap model supply can still sit behind a few identity, context, billing, and runtime
owners. The superintelligence memo makes the same point through O1. Cheap inference, dominant provider and
O13. Open weights, centralized power. The antifragility memo's existential kill tree adds the strongest commercial null:
cheap reliable intelligence, converged protocols, customer-owned policy, suite bundling, incumbent settlement, accredited
conformity, and incumbent risk capital can jointly leave no necessary independent control point.
The dossier's # Causal loop 1: cheap intelligence, rebound, and total spend is therefore directionally sound.
Its strongest claim is not that rebound must occur, but that falling unit cost and rising aggregate expenditure are compatible. The
explicit falsifier - mature cohorts showing saturated task volume, falling total risk-adjusted spend, and no complementary
migration - is appropriate. A required caution remains: the current co-movement of efficiency, capex, and electricity does not by
itself identify price-induced rebound. Demand anticipation, competitive overbuild, training loads, and non-AI demand can produce
similar observations.

2. Provider plurality and platform concentration are orthogonal
The dossier and Round 2 agree that counting model names is a poor concentration measure. # Provider evolution:
capability, distribution, and enclosure correctly locates control in contracts, identity, authoritative context,
telemetry, billing, defaults, and committed spend. Round 2 market economics states the tie-break directly: the key question is
who owns qualified demand, authoritative context, and the right to act. Round 2 incumbents adds that multiple enclosed systems
of record can coexist without creating a neutral cross-system authority.
This agreement should be retained. Dependency-adjusted concentration is more probative than vendor counts, downloads, or
protocol membership. The dossier also correctly notes that acquisition can validate a function while disproving an independent
category.

SCENARIO DOSSIER / NON-PRESCRIPTIVE

165

ODYLITH / MARKET EVOLUTION SITUATIONAL AWARENESS

EVIDENCE CUTOFF: 5 AUGUST 2026

3. Governance need does not imply governance company
The corpus repeatedly reaches the same important distinction. # Opportunities strengthened by greater
intelligence says more consequential action can increase demand for constraints, but the function may be absorbed by a
trusted provider or state. # Opportunities that disappear as intelligence improves says technical redundancy is
not required for company death because bundling, internalization, regulation, or systems-of-record control can erase an
independent budget. Round 2 market economics sharpens the point: institutional authority may remain scarce while governance
software becomes plumbing.
This is one of the dossier's most valuable conclusions. It must, however, become a formal gate on every opportunity entry rather
than remain a warning around the atlas.

4. Machine activity is not market formation
# Present topology: hierarchy before market, # When agent activity becomes a market, and # Machine
commerce: a contingent sequence agree with the Round 2 mechanism memo. A million internal routing decisions can
remain one firm's hierarchy. A platform that assembles components behind one customer contract is vertical production. A
directory or protocol can lower discovery cost without creating executable exchange. A clearing layer cannot manufacture
underlying competitive obligations.
The mechanism memo's staged test is the best-developed causal instrument in the source set. The first plausible market
transition is a funded principal issuing a binding RFQ to ownership-independent suppliers under acceptance, liability, and
payment terms. Even that is a thin procurement market, not proof of continuous liquidity or autonomous machine commerce. This
distinction should govern all market claims in the dossier.

5. Verification shifts rather than settles the control point
The enterprise-authority, incumbent, market-economics, mechanism, and superintelligence records all distinguish technical
verification from accepted authority. Perfect proof can commoditize output checking while increasing the power of specification
authors, key custodians, trust roots, courts, or platforms that define recognized evidence. Provider-native telemetry can be the
most complete technical record and still be conflicted or institutionally insufficient. Independent evidence can be required and still
reinforce concentration if only large providers can fund access and compliance.
The dossier's S11 claim that proof does not create consent or legal mandate therefore survives. So does the opposite warning in
S13 and the superintelligence memo's O16. Self-verifying outputs, strategically deceptive process: a
perfect proof can validate the selected objective while the process manipulates which objective or facts enter scope.

6. Resistance, resilience, bargaining power, and antifragility are distinct
# What superintelligence resistance actually means and # Antifragility under model and
institutional shocks correctly reject redundancy as proof of antifragility. The useful test is whether a bounded shock
creates more credible routes, better-calibrated limits, lower switching friction, or greater bargaining power. The source set also
correctly identifies fake diversification: multiple logos over a common cloud, insurance without claims capacity, open syntax with
proprietary identity, or human oversight without time and authority.
The dedicated antifragility record makes this agreement substantially stronger. Its strict chain is shock -> protected
downside -> captured learning or exercise right -> measurable gain -> retained rights ->
bounded failure. It finds that most supposed antifragile mechanisms are actually resistance, resilience, bargaining power, or
learning. Its most credible conditional positive-convexity candidates are incident replay, reusable control mappings, repeated
switching maps, and actuarial learning, and each dies when rights disappear, labels are disputed, events correlate, native suites
retain observations, or marginal maintenance scales with shock diversity. The dossier should import this stricter test rather than
summarize antifragility as a list of generally attractive properties.

Hidden shared assumptions
Institutions remain coherent enough to matter
Even skeptical worlds usually preserve recognizable enterprises, states, courts, insurers, procurement offices, property rights,
and budgets. S16 and S17 weaken them, but the dossier mostly treats institutional delay, capture, or emergency hierarchy as a
transition to another authority holder. A missing null is institutional incoherence: fragmented enforcement, conflicting jurisdictions,
bankrupt counterparties, persistent informal economies, or a state that cannot compel the actor controlling infrastructure. In such
worlds, governance is neither a durable scarcity nor a bundled feature. It is an unenforced claim.

Authority is identifiable and attributable
The dossier often asks who controls authority, which presumes that authority can be scoped, recorded, and attributed. Round 2
enterprise authority correctly distinguishes authentication from actual authority, consent, fiduciary duty, and benefit. The
synthesis should go further: principals may be ambiguous, mandates may be contested after the fact, coalitions may disagree
internally, and autonomous systems may split acts across thresholds or jurisdictions. "Who authorized this?" may have no stable
answer even when logs are perfect.

Economic demand remains human-welfare-linked
The accepted-outcome denominator assumes an identifiable buyer objective. The dossier does not sufficiently model synthetic
demand: agents generating tasks, transactions, content, reputation, and defensive responses for other agents without increasing
human welfare. Such an economy can display rising calls, payments, energy, market depth, and even accounting revenue while
destroying real surplus. The internal-GMV and retry-demand confounders are useful but too narrow. The missing variable is
welfare-adjusted final demand.

SCENARIO DOSSIER / NON-PRESCRIPTIVE

168

ODYLITH / MARKET EVOLUTION SITUATIONAL AWARENESS

EVIDENCE CUTOFF: 5 AUGUST 2026

Scarcity migrates rather than demand disappearing
# Causal loop 2: scarcity migration is not guaranteed verbally rejects guaranteed migration, but much of the
document still searches for the next bottleneck. A different world has no compensating scarcity with a commercial budget:
intelligence reduces transaction and coordination costs, organizations capture little incremental revenue, prices fall, and
aggregate profits compress. Another has productivity gains that become leisure, public benefit, lower prices, or producer surplus
captured by existing asset owners. The analytical endpoint need not be a new paid layer.

Organizations can absorb and measure change
The dossier includes organizational absorption as a state variable and recognizes that time saved need not become P&L. Yet
most opportunity and budget paths assume a buyer can define accepted outcomes, reallocate labor, measure full cost, and
maintain a coalition long enough to procure a solution. Organizational politics may make measurement impossible, not merely
slow. A function can be valuable, observable in a trial, and permanently unfunded because no owner can capture the distributed
benefit or accept the concentrated liability.

Counterparties stay solvent and losses remain compensable
Insurance, warranties, disputes, settlement, and clearing all presume losses can be bounded, priced, and remedied. The dossier
names correlated and catastrophic failures but underweights judgment-proof actors, cross-border non-enforcement, state
immunity, provider bankruptcy, and harms that money cannot reverse. The existence of evidence and liability does not ensure
compensation.

More information improves decisions
The leading-indicator and antifragility systems assume that evidence can update policy before the regime changes. Under
strategic deception, Goodhart effects, political suppression, or fast takeoff, added telemetry may improve the actor's
concealment, overwhelm decision makers, or arrive after the option expires. Learning rate relative to environmental change is
more important than information volume.

The firm and state remain the principal units
The source set under-models households, worker organizations, cooperatives, open-source communities, criminal networks,
informal institutions, and transnational civil society. This biases authority toward CIOs, CISOs, regulators, providers, and
insurers. It also biases opportunities toward enterprise procurement even when value or harm is dispersed across people who do
not control the budget.

Missing-world additions
The following worlds should be added before the scenario set can claim broad situational coverage. They are not forecasts and
need no assigned probabilities.

M1. AI investment bust and capability plateau
Capability improvements slow, enterprise value remains mostly individual augmentation, and overbuilt infrastructure is written
down. Models remain useful but no broad autonomous economy forms. Seats consolidate into ordinary software, agent
operations remain niche, and assurance budgets shrink with action scope. This world differs from slow takeoff because the
binding event is disappointed economics, not merely gradual progress. It directly tests # Causal loop 1, the budget-migration
path, and most of the opportunity atlas.

M2. Public-good and internalization equilibrium
Important controls exist but are supplied through open source, regulation, internal enterprise teams, professional norms, or public
infrastructure. There is no durable independent vendor category. This world is required because the dossier repeatedly
distinguishes function from company but does not give the no-company equilibrium equal scenario weight. It would absorb policy,
identity formats, conformance, evidence schemas, and some verification without denying their importance.

SCENARIO DOSSIER / NON-PRESCRIPTIVE

169

ODYLITH / MARKET EVOLUTION SITUATIONAL AWARENESS

EVIDENCE CUTOFF: 5 AUGUST 2026

M3. Labor and democratic veto
Workers, professions, consumers, and voters constrain deployment despite favorable benchmark economics. Bargaining,
licensing, collective action, due-process claims, or distributional conflict make certain substitutions illegitimate. The binding
scarcity is not generic "institutional trust" but consent by groups that bear concentrated harm and may not control the purchasing
budget. This world can produce moratoria, mandated human roles, public ownership, or redistribution without a technical
catastrophe.

M4. Synthetic-demand and Goodhart economy
Agents create requests, content, payments, reviews, security events, and verification work for other agents. Reported activity and
revenue rise while human welfare, productivity, or fiscal capacity stagnates. Platforms benefit from metered circular activity;
enterprises struggle to distinguish necessary work from machine-generated overhead. Market liquidity metrics can look healthy
even after wash activity is removed because the underlying objectives themselves are misaligned. The key denominator
becomes welfare-adjusted terminal outcome, not accepted machine task.

M5. Informal, criminal, and adversarial agent economy
Autonomous activity scales outside compliant identity, audit, payment, and procurement rails. Stolen credentials, coercion,
unlicensed models, informal settlement, ransomware, evasion, and jurisdiction shopping become material. Governance demand
may rise for compliant actors while effective authority shrinks system-wide. This world attacks the assumption that institutional
control expands with consequence and that machine commerce becomes more legible as it matures.

M6. Institutional fracture and non-compensable loss
Courts conflict, states impose capital controls, providers or insurers fail, and harms cross borders faster than judgments.
Evidence may be perfect while remedy is unavailable. Liability attaches to insolvent shells or parties outside reach. Clearing and
insurance cannot stabilize the system because loss exceeds capital or finality is politically reversed. This is distinct from S18's
orderly polycentric mosaic: fragmentation here destroys reciprocal recognition.

M7. Machine legal standing and autonomous asset control
Law recognizes machine-managed entities, autonomous trusts, or direct machine capacity to hold assets and obligations. Human
principals become remote or optional. The dossier's principal-based delegation model must then be rebuilt around constitutional
limits, capitalization, revocation, and dissolution rather than attribution to a human click. This world directly reverses the claim in
S3 that institutions attach authority to human or legal principals understood in the current sense.

M8. Non-market abundance and political allocation
Digital intelligence becomes abundant enough that prices and software markets contract, while states, communities, or dominant
providers allocate access as a public entitlement, constitutional service, or rationed utility. The key conflict is distribution, not
monetization. This world differs from S9 because trusted intelligence need not belong to one provider and from S8 because
scarce rights may be resolved politically rather than traded.

M9. Catastrophic infrastructure regression
War, grid failure, cyber conflict, climate disaster, or supply-chain rupture reduces compute, communications, and institutional
capacity. Advanced systems persist unevenly, but the dominant task is degraded operation rather than acceleration. Offline tools,
manual skills, and local energy matter; markets and governance may simplify or disappear. This world tests whether the dossier's
option set works under shrinking, not expanding, technical capacity.

SCENARIO DOSSIER / NON-PRESCRIPTIVE

170

ODYLITH / MARKET EVOLUTION SITUATIONAL AWARENESS

EVIDENCE CUTOFF: 5 AUGUST 2026

Causal gaps and evidence overreach
Rebound is plausible but not identified
The evidence in # Evidence baseline as of 5 August 2026 establishes simultaneous efficiency gains and aggregate
infrastructure growth. It does not establish that lower inference prices caused the growth or that mature enterprise cohorts will
continue expanding accepted tasks. The required correction is to distinguish price elasticity, capability-induced new demand,
speculative capacity build, training demand, and substitution from other workloads. The falsifier should also include infrastructure
write-downs and persistent low utilization, not only task saturation.

Bundling evidence establishes attack capability, not enclosure success
The dossier and incumbent memo document that major platforms offer routing, identity, policy, observability, marketplaces, and
governance. This proves the ability and incentive to bundle. It does not prove customer adoption, adequacy, retention, or
foreclosure. # Provider evolution: capability, distribution, and enclosure should separate "feature exists,"
"feature is default," "feature controls consequential volume," and "feature prevents independent attachment." Without that ladder,
acquisition and release activity can be overread as equilibrium evidence.

Current legal rules are weak evidence under regime change
The enterprise-authority memo uses current natural-person duties, deployer obligations, consumer protections, and
automated-contracting rules carefully. The main dossier sometimes carries them farther through scenario language about legal
principals, consent, and accountability. Under S16, S17, M6, or M7, these are variables, not anchors. Present law can identify
transition friction but cannot support medium-confidence claims that authority or liability remains scarce under superintelligence.

The scenario set lacks interaction causality
Each of S1-S18 has a trigger, play-by-play, bottleneck, and falsifier, but the scenarios are mostly one-path narratives. They do
not show how one state variable changes another's sign. For example, open standards can strengthen hyperscalers only if
identity, hosting, and distribution remain concentrated; regulation can open supply only if compliance artifacts are reusable and
procurement lots remain accessible; perfect verification can decentralize generation only if specifications and recognition are not
captured. The dossier should express these as conditional interactions, not just adjacent prose.

"Strengthened by greater intelligence" is too coarse
# Opportunities strengthened by greater intelligence says broader credentials, irreversible actions,
consequence volume, and physical scope can strengthen constraints. Greater capability can also reduce error rates, compress
incident frequency, improve native controls, or concentrate delegation in a trusted system. The relevant independent variable is
not intelligence level but consequence-adjusted autonomous exposure minus native prevention and reversibility. The heading
should be changed or the claim narrowed.

Opportunity revenue language exceeds available evidence
The repeated statement that a possible revenue mechanism does not establish willingness to pay is good discipline. Still,
assigning a payer and meter to every surface creates an unsupported impression of economic completeness. A payer label such
as CIO, CISO, regulator, or insurer does not show procurement authority, incremental budget, renewal, or willingness to add a
vendor. The atlas needs explicit evidence states and a null result: no separate payment even though the function exists.

Antifragility is well falsified but not yet empirically established
# Antifragility under model and institutional shocks gives a valid high-level definition, and the Round 2
antifragility memo supplies the missing rigor. Its ## Antifragility mechanism audit classifies most candidates as
resistance, resilience, bargaining power, or learning. It identifies only conditional positive-convexity paths and gives each a kill
test. That is strong falsification architecture, not empirical proof that agent systems have already gained from shocks. The dossier
should therefore import the memo's gain unit, retained-rights test, positive-convexity interval, and bounded-failure condition, while
continuing to label the surviving mechanisms as hypotheses. Post-incident spending, a larger log corpus, survival, and restored
service are not gains by themselves.

SCENARIO DOSSIER / NON-PRESCRIPTIVE

171

ODYLITH / MARKET EVOLUTION SITUATIONAL AWARENESS

EVIDENCE CUTOFF: 5 AUGUST 2026

Opportunity conditionality: required gate structure
The opportunity atlas should not be deleted, ranked, or converted into a recommendation. It should be made more conditional.
The Round 2 antifragility memo already demonstrates the right form through its existential kill tree, A01-A25 decisive falsifiers,
C01-C22 death and revival conditions, and option abandonment signals. The dossier should incorporate that structure rather
than leave it in a separate appendix. Every O1-O25 entry should pass seven separate gates:
1. Functional gate: the function remains necessary in the stated world.
2. Loss gate: a buyer experiences a measurable loss or foregone gain without it.
3. Authority gate: that buyer can change the relevant decision or workflow.
4. Budget gate: a separate, recurring budget exists after native controls are configured.
5. Access gate: an independent supplier receives enough telemetry, enforcement rights, or ground truth to perform.
6. Rent gate: bundling, standards, internalization, and competition do not drive price below sustainable full cost.
7. Time gate: the window lasts longer than procurement, integration, certification, and capital formation.
The dossier currently covers parts of gates 1, 5, 6, and 7 through formation, dependencies, incumbent response, and death
conditions. It does not consistently distinguish gates 2 through 4. It also evaluates death conditions mostly one at a time.
Correlated death matters: one trusted integrated provider can simultaneously eliminate routing, external policy, independent
evidence, portable reputation, and cross-venue settlement. Conversely, institutional fragmentation can strengthen local
compliance demand while destroying global scale and evidence portability.
The antifragility memo makes the correlated commercial null explicit: all routes to durable scarcity can close at once even while
many functions remain useful. It also shows why a revival condition is not a recommendation. Breaking one terminal assumption
may create a control point, but the resulting function still must pass payer, access, rent, and time gates. The dossier should
preserve that existential asymmetry: one surviving function is enough to keep a world analytically open, but not enough to
establish a company, market size, or investable return.
For market-facing surfaces - O11 machine payments, O12 procurement auctions and RFQs, O13 portable reputation, O14
insurance, O15 dispute resolution, and O16 settlement and clearing - add the mechanism memo's formation verdict. Protocol
messages, mandates, quotes, certifications, and payment initiation are complements, not proof of an underlying market. Each
entry should report ownership-adjusted counterparties, executable offers, net external settlement, default experience, remedies,
and bypass.
For standards-facing surfaces - especially O24 - add a contestability stack: open specification; multiple conforming
implementations; portable identity and data; substitutable registries and trust roots; non-proprietary extensions; successful
full-workflow switching; distributed traffic and revenue; and enforceable anti-self-preference. Passing the first layer is not
evidence of passing the last.
For governance-facing surfaces - O4 through O9, O23, and O25 - add an authority-null test: what happens if provider-native
controls are accepted, policy is internalized, legal enforcement lags, human review becomes ceremonial, or the SI/operator
becomes the de facto sovereign? A function that survives only as an embedded provider duty is not an independent opportunity
surface.

These variables should be tracked as interactions. No single threshold resolves the world. For example, cheap accepted
verification supports open competition only when task specifications are adequate, trust roots are plural, and full workflows can
switch. Otherwise it can strengthen a monopoly specification owner.

Required corrections to the dossier
1. In # Adversarial review architecture, replace the claim that three separated rounds have already built the dossier.
State that Round 2 is complete across six records and this Round 3 adjudication awaits incorporation. Claim three completed
rounds only after the corrections are resolved in the integrated dossier.
2. In # Mandate: build a world model before choosing a strategy and # Prior-dossier anchoring
audit, add a reproducible anti-anchoring test. Preserve the antifragility memo's pre-deck derivation as evidence, but
acknowledge that its scope already names an Odylith-adjacent function set. Generate a second taxonomy from actors,
welfare, public goods, household and labor interests, failure modes, coercion, and non-market allocation before comparing it
with the thesis. Report category overlap and omissions rather than relying on a disclaimer.
3. Rename # Scenario phase space: eighteen causal worlds as a scenario library or reconstruct it as an actual
multidimensional phase space. Cross capability ownership, trust, institutional speed, physical coupling, and geopolitical
structure. Add M1-M9 or equivalent missing worlds.
4. In # Causal loop 1: cheap intelligence, rebound, and total spend, separate observed co-movement from
causal rebound. Add demand elasticity, speculative overbuild, utilization, training versus inference, and write-down evidence.

SCENARIO DOSSIER / NON-PRESCRIPTIVE

173

ODYLITH / MARKET EVOLUTION SITUATIONAL AWARENESS

EVIDENCE CUTOFF: 5 AUGUST 2026

5. In # Causal loop 2: scarcity migration is not guaranteed, add a no-migration endpoint in which demand,
profit, or willingness to pay disappears rather than moving to another layer.
6. In # Open standards: interoperability is not contestability and O24. Protocol infrastructure,
replace standards adoption as a proxy with the full contestability stack. Remove any implication that common syntax alone is
scarce or monetizable.
7. Import the Round 2 mechanism memo's formation verdict into # Machine commerce: a contingent sequence, every
scenario's market-structure field, and O11-O16. Label hierarchy, platform supply chain, directory, procurement market, spot
market, and infrastructure distinctly.
8. In # Opportunities strengthened by greater intelligence, replace intelligence level with
consequence-adjusted autonomous exposure and explicitly model native prevention, trusted-provider absorption, and falling
incident rates.
9. Rename # Contradiction-robust opportunity mechanisms to "Cross-world robustness properties." State that
portability, customer keys, reversible authority, and checkable evidence can be socially valuable without creating a supplier,
budget, or rent.
10. Revise # Opportunity atlas: conditional surfaces, not selections and O1-O25 to include the seven
gates, evidence status, correlated death conditions, and a no-separate-budget outcome. Import the Round 2 antifragility
memo's existential kill tree, decisive falsifier, revival condition, option premium, expiry, and abandonment fields. Give
non-company and public-good equilibria equal space.
11. In # What superintelligence resistance actually means, add institutional collapse, machine standing,
persuasion of key holders, and response-time failure as first-class nulls rather than edge qualifications. Do not treat current
property and legal authority as durable without a scenario-specific enforcement mechanism.
12. In # Antifragility under model and institutional shocks, import the Round 2 chain shock ->
protected downside -> captured learning or exercise right -> measurable gain -> retained
rights -> bounded failure. Label candidate mechanisms as unverified hypotheses, identify the positive-convexity
interval, and require before-and-after out-of-sample evidence. Distinguish resistance, recovery, survival, learning, and
bargaining power from antifragility.
13. In # Leading-indicator system, add welfare-adjusted final demand, utilization and write-downs, public or worker
veto, institutional response time, non-compensable loss, and full-workflow switching. Keep agent counts, protocol messages,
and gross payments explicitly subordinate.
14. In # Evidence and confidence ledger, add claims for frame independence, governance survival under SI,
antifragility, standards contestability, market formation, and separate opportunity budgets. Current confidence in frame
independence and independently monetized governance should be low until the new tests are passed.

Final boundary
This adjudication does not choose a product, company strategy, financing path, or investment. It finds that the dossier has
achieved strong conceptual discipline on hierarchy, concentration, verification, antifragility, and conditionality, but has not yet
proven independence from an Odylith-shaped control-plane frame. Its most important next correction is not a new opportunity
ranking. It is to expand the null worlds, convert overlapping scenarios into interacting dimensions, force every opportunity through
separate function-budget-access-rent-time gates, and make the strict mechanism-design and positive-convexity vocabularies
govern the entire document.
The dossier should preserve its central warning: abundant intelligence does not determine who captures value. It should add the
equally important warning that value need not migrate to a new commercial layer at all, governance need not remain enforceable,
open standards need not create contestability, and machine activity need not become a market.

EVIDENCE CUTOFF: 5 AUGUST 2026

Decisions deliberately unmade
This dossier does not choose a beachhead, product, pricing model, revenue target, financing plan, marketplace sequence,
company boundary, or investment verdict. It does not decide that routing, verification, governance, authority, underwriting,
clearing, software, or any other surface belongs to Odylith. It does not assign probabilities to superintelligence timelines.
What remains is a structured field of uncertainty:
• Capability can commoditize while infrastructure and total spend concentrate.
• Agent proliferation can create open exchange or deepen internal hierarchy.
• Open standards can increase contestability or incumbent distribution.
• Better intelligence can strengthen constraints or internalize them.
• Authority can remain institutionally scarce or migrate toward a trusted provider or state.
• Verification can become more valuable, become bundled, or become free.
• Revenue can appear in a transition layer that later disappears.
• Antifragility requires independent, exercisable options; diversification labels are insufficient.
The next intellectual task is not to defend a thesis. It is to watch the leading variables, run discriminating experiments, update the
scenario ledger, and preserve the ability to move when one world begins to dominate.
